.

We prove this lemma by applying the Stein’s Lemma, which states that
\mathbb{E}[x\cdot g(x)]=\mathbb{E}[\nabla g(x)]
holds for
x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
and any differentiable
g
.

Step 1: Decomposition using Stein’s Lemma.

To apply Stein’s Lemma, we rewrite the
\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{p}^{\top}XX^{\top}p]
into the summation of a sequence of functions of Gaussian random variables:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{p}^{\top}XX^{\top}p]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\big\langle X^{\top}p,X^{\top}\widetilde{p}\big\rangle\bigr]=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\langle\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}p_{\ell}\cdot x_{\ell},\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}\cdot x_{\ell}\biggr\rangle\biggr]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}[\|x_{\ell}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}]+\sum_{1\leq\ell\neq k\leq L}\mathbb{E}[\langle x_{\ell},x_{k}\rangle\cdot p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{k}].

(E.1)

Here we let
p_{\ell}
and
\widetilde{p}_{\ell}
denote the
\ell
-th entry of softmax vectors
p
and
\widetilde{p}
, respectively.
In the sequel, for any
j\in[d]
and any
\ell\in[L]
, we let
x_{\ell,j}
denote the
j
-th entry of
x_{\ell}
.
For notational simplicity, we let
\partial_{\ell,j}
denote as the partial derivative of function with respect to
x_{\ell,j}
.
By applying the Stein’s Lemma twice, we can show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|x_{\ell}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}]+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[x_{\ell,j}\cdot p_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}]+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[x_{\ell,j}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}p_{\ell}]

\displaystyle=d\cdot\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}]+2\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{\ell,j}p_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}]+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}^{2}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}]+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{p}_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}^{2}p_{\ell}].

(E.2)

Here the first equality is obtained by applying Stein’s lemma with
g(x_{\ell,j})=x_{\ell,j}\cdot p_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{\ell}
.
Similarly,
for any
k\neq\ell
, by applying Stein’s Lemma twice,
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\langle x_{\ell},x_{k}\rangle\cdot p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{k}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[x_{k,j}\cdot p_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}\widetilde{p}_{k}]+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[x_{k,j}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{k}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}p_{\ell}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{k,j}\partial_{\ell,j}\widetilde{p}_{k}]+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{p}_{k}\cdot\partial_{k,j}\partial_{\ell,j}p_{\ell}]

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{k,j}p_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}\widetilde{p}_{k}]+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{k,j}\widetilde{p}_{k}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}p_{\ell}].

(E.3)

Step 2: Derivatives Calculations and Simplifications.

By (
E.2
) and (
E.3
),
we write (
E.1
) as a sum of expectations involving
p
,
\widetilde{p}
, and first- and second-order their derivatives.
By direct calculations, the first-order derivatives of
p
are given by

\displaystyle\partial_{\ell,j}p_{\ell}=\omega\cdot\upsilon_{j}\cdot(p_{\ell}-p^{2}_{\ell}),\qquad\partial_{k,j}p_{\ell}=-\omega\cdot\upsilon_{j}\cdot p_{\ell}p_{k}.

(E.4)

The second-order derivatives of
p
are given by

\displaystyle\partial_{\ell,j}^{2}p_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\omega\cdot\upsilon_{j}\cdot(1-2p_{\ell})\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}p_{\ell}=\omega^{2}\cdot\upsilon_{j}^{2}\cdot(1-2p_{\ell})\cdot(p_{\ell}-p^{2}_{\ell}),

(E.5)

\displaystyle\partial_{k,j}\partial_{\ell,j}p_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\ell,j}\partial_{k,j}p_{\ell}=\omega\cdot\upsilon_{j}\cdot(1-2p_{\ell})\cdot\partial_{k,j}p_{\ell}=-\omega^{2}\cdot\upsilon_{j}^{2}\cdot(1-2p_{\ell})\cdot p_{\ell}p_{k}.

(E.6)

We can similarly derive the first- and second-order derivatives of
\widetilde{p}
.

In the following, we conclude the proof of this lemma by substituting the calculations above back into (
E.2
) and (
E.3
), and simplifing the expression.
First, note that, if we view
\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{p}^{\top}XX^{\top}p]
as a bivariate function of
(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})
, it is a quadratic function.
The constant term is given by

d\cdot\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}]=d\cdot\mathbb{E}[p^{\top}\widetilde{p}].

(E.7)

Thus, we can write
\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{p}^{\top}XX^{\top}p]
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{p}^{\top}XX^{\top}p]=A_{0}\cdot\omega^{2}+A_{1}\cdot\omega\cdot\widetilde{\omega}+A_{2}\cdot\widetilde{\omega}^{2}+d\cdot\mathbb{E}[p^{\top}\widetilde{p}]

(E.8)

for some constants
A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}
to be determined.
By examining the derivatives of
p
and
\widetilde{p}
, we notice that these coefficients are given respectively by

\displaystyle A_{0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{p}_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}^{2}p_{\ell}]+\sum_{1\leq\ell\neq k\leq L}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{p}_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{k,j}\partial_{\ell,j}p_{k}],

(E.9)

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{\ell,j}p_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}]+\sum_{1\leq\ell\neq k\leq L}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\bigl\{\mathbb{E}[\partial_{k,j}p_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}\widetilde{p}_{k}]+\mathbb{E}[\partial_{k,j}\widetilde{p}_{k}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}p_{\ell}]\bigr\},

(E.10)

\displaystyle A_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}^{2}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}]+\sum_{1\leq\ell\neq k\leq L}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{k,j}\partial_{\ell,j}\widetilde{p}_{k}].

(E.11)

For
A_{2}
, combining (
E.5
), (
E.6
), and (
E.11
), we have

\displaystyle A_{2}=\widetilde{\omega}^{2}\cdot\|\upsilon\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\left(\mathbb{E}[p^{\top}\widetilde{p}]-2\cdot\mathbb{E}[p^{\top}\widetilde{p}^{\odot 2}]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\widetilde{\omega}^{2}\cdot\|\upsilon\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\bigg\{\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{\ell}]+\sum_{1\leq\ell\neq k\leq L}\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{k}]\bigg\}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\widetilde{\omega}^{2}\cdot\|\upsilon\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\bigg\{\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{\ell}^{2}]+\sum_{1\leq\ell\neq k\leq L}\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{k}^{2}]\bigg\}.

By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{\ell}]+\sum_{1\leq\ell\neq k\leq L}\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{k}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}\bigg)\cdot\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{L}\widetilde{p}_{k}\bigg)\bigg]=\mathbb{E}[p^{\top}\widetilde{p}],

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{\ell}^{2}]+\sum_{1\leq\ell\neq k\leq L}\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{k}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}\bigg)\cdot\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{L}\widetilde{p}_{k}^{2}\bigg)\bigg]=\mathbb{E}[p^{\top}\widetilde{p}\cdot\|\widetilde{p}\|_{2}^{2}],

where in the first equality, we use the fact that
\widetilde{p}
is a prabability distribution.
Combining the above three equalities, we can simplify
A_{2}
as

\displaystyle A_{2}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\omega}^{2}\cdot\|\upsilon\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\left(\mathbb{E}[p^{\top}\widetilde{p}]-2\cdot\mathbb{E}[p^{\top}\widetilde{p}^{\odot 2}]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\widetilde{\omega}^{2}\cdot\|\upsilon\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[p^{\top}\widetilde{p}\cdot\widetilde{p}^{\top}{\bm{1}}_{L}]+2\omega^{2}\cdot\|\upsilon\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[p^{\top}\widetilde{p}\cdot\|\widetilde{p}\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle=2\widetilde{\omega}^{2}\cdot\|\upsilon\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[-p^{\top}\widetilde{p}^{\odot 2}+p^{\top}\widetilde{p}\cdot\|\widetilde{p}\|_{2}^{2}].

(E.12)

Following the same argument, we can show that
A_{0}
defined in (
E.9
) can be simplified as

\displaystyle A_{0}=2\omega^{2}\cdot\|\upsilon\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[-\widetilde{p}^{\top}p^{\odot 2}+p^{\top}\widetilde{p}\cdot\|p\|_{2}^{2}].

(E.13)

Then, it remains to consider
A_{1}
. Note that by the first equality in (
E.4
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{\ell,j}p_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}]

\displaystyle=\omega\widetilde{\omega}\cdot\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\upsilon_{j}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{\ell}-p^{2}_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{\ell}-p_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}^{2}_{\ell}+p^{2}_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}^{2}_{\ell}]

\displaystyle=\omega\widetilde{\omega}\cdot\|\upsilon\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[p^{\top}\widetilde{p}-p^{\top}\widetilde{p}^{\odot 2}-\widetilde{p}^{\top}p^{\odot 2}]+\omega\widetilde{\omega}\cdot\|\upsilon\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}\left[p^{2}_{\ell}\widetilde{p}^{2}_{\ell}\right].

By the second equality in (
E.4
), for any
k\neq\ell
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\partial_{k,j}\widetilde{p}_{k}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}p_{\ell}]

\displaystyle=\omega\widetilde{\omega}\cdot v_{j}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}p_{k}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{k}]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\partial_{k,j}\widetilde{p}_{k}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}p_{\ell}]

\displaystyle=\omega\widetilde{\omega}\cdot v_{j}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{\ell}-p_{\ell}^{2}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{\ell}-p_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}^{2}_{\ell}+p^{2}_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}^{2}_{\ell}].

Thus, combining (
E.10
) with the above three equalities, we can simplify the first part of
A_{1}
as

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{\ell,j}p_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}]+\sum_{1\leq\ell\neq k\leq L}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{k,j}p_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}\widetilde{p}_{k}]

\displaystyle\quad=\omega\widetilde{\omega}\cdot\|\upsilon\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[p^{\top}\widetilde{p}-p^{\top}\widetilde{p}^{\odot 2}-\widetilde{p}^{\top}p^{\odot 2}]

\displaystyle\qquad+\omega\widetilde{\omega}\cdot\|\upsilon\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\bigg\{\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}\left[(p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{\ell})^{2}\right]+\sum_{1\leq\ell\neq k\leq L}\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}\cdot p_{k}\widetilde{p}_{k}]\bigg\}

\displaystyle\qquad=\omega\widetilde{\omega}\cdot\|\upsilon\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[p^{\top}\widetilde{p}-p^{\top}\widetilde{p}^{\odot 2}-\widetilde{p}^{\top}p^{\odot 2}+(p^{\top}\widetilde{p})^{2}].

(E.14)

Similarly, the second part of
A_{1}
can be written as

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{\ell,j}p_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}\widetilde{p}_{\ell}]+\sum_{1\leq\ell\neq k\leq L}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{k,j}\widetilde{p}_{k}\cdot\partial_{\ell,j}p_{\ell}]

\displaystyle\quad=\omega\widetilde{\omega}\cdot\|\upsilon\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{\ell}-p_{\ell}^{2}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{\ell}-p_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}^{2}_{\ell}+p^{2}_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}^{2}_{\ell}]

\displaystyle\qquad+\omega\widetilde{\omega}\cdot\|\upsilon\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\sum_{1\leq\ell\neq k\leq L}\mathbb{E}[p_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{k}-p^{2}_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{k}-p_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{p}^{2}_{k}+p_{\ell}^{2}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{k}^{2}]

\displaystyle\quad=\omega\widetilde{\omega}\cdot\|\upsilon\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[p^{\top}{\bm{1}}_{L}\cdot\widetilde{p}^{\top}{\bm{1}}_{L}-\|p\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\widetilde{p}^{\top}{\bm{1}}_{L}-\|\widetilde{p}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot p^{\top}{\bm{1}}_{L}+\|p\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\|\widetilde{p}\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle\quad=\omega\widetilde{\omega}\cdot\|\upsilon\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[(1-\|p\|_{2}^{2})\cdot(1-\|\widetilde{p}\|_{2}^{2})].

(E.15)

Thus, combining (
E.14
) and (
E.15
), we can write
A_{1}
as

\displaystyle A_{1}=\omega\widetilde{\omega}\cdot\|\upsilon\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigl[p^{\top}\widetilde{p}-p^{\top}\widetilde{p}^{\odot 2}-\widetilde{p}^{\top}p^{\odot 2}+(p^{\top}\widetilde{p})^{2}+(1-\|p\|_{2}^{2})\cdot(1-\|\widetilde{p}\|_{2}^{2})\bigr].

(E.16)

Finally, by combining (
E.7
), (
E.8
), (
E.12
), (
E.13
), and (
E.16
), we complete the proof.
∎

E.2
Proof of Concentration Arguments

Lemma E.1

(
\chi^{2}
-concentration)
.

Consider a random vector
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with each entry i.i.d. sampled from
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. For any positive
t>0
, the following inequality holds:

\mathbb{P}(\|x\|_{2}^{2}\geq d+2\sqrt{dt}+2t)\leq\exp(-t),\quad\mathbb{P}(\|x\|_{2}^{2}\leq d-2\sqrt{dt})\leq\exp(-t).

Also, it holds that

\mathbb{P}(\|x\|_{2}^{2}\geq d\cdot(1+t)^{2})\leq\exp(-dt^{2}/2).

Proof of Lemma
E.1
.

See Lemma 1 in
Laurent and Massart (2000)
and Lemma A.1 in
Bai et al. (2024)
for detailed proofs.
∎

Lemma E.2

(Binomial concentration)
.

Consider Binomial random variable
X\sim{\rm Binom}(n,p)
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
p\in(0,1)
, then for any
t\in(0,1)
, it holds that

\mathbb{P}(X\geq(1+t)\cdot np)\leq\exp(-t^{2}np/3),\quad\mathbb{P}(X\geq(1-t)\cdot np)\leq\exp(-t^{2}np/2).

Proof of Lemma
E.2
.

The result follows a direct implementation of Chernoff’s bound.
∎

Lemma E.3
.

Consider
x_{\ell}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
,
\beta\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d}/d)
and
y_{\ell}=\beta^{\top}x_{\ell}+\epsilon_{\ell}
, where
\epsilon_{\ell}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
with
L\in\mathbb{N}
. For any
\delta\in(0,1)
, with probability at least
1-\delta
, the following events hold simultaneously:

(i).

\max_{\ell\in[L]}|x_{\ell}^{\top}x_{q}|\leq\sqrt{12d}\cdot\log(8L/\delta)
;

(ii).

\frac{1}{L}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}y_{\ell}^{2}\leq 1+\sigma^{2}+{\rm poly}(d^{-1/2},L^{-1/2},\log(1/\delta))
;

(iii).

\big|\frac{1}{L}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\ind(x_{\ell}^{\top}x_{q}>0)-\frac{1}{2}\big|\leq\min\{\sqrt{\log(8/\delta)/L},1/2\}
.

Proof of Lemma
E.3
.

We first consider the following good events hold:

\mathcal{E}_{1}=\left\{\|x_{q}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{6d\log(4/\delta)},\quad\|\beta\|_{2}\leq 1+\sqrt{2d^{-1}\cdot\log(4/\delta)}\right\},

and we can upper bound
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1})\leq\delta/4
using Lemma
E.1
.
Note that for fixed
x_{q}
, we have
x_{\ell}^{\top}x_{q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,\|x_{q}\|_{2}^{2})
and hence by applying a Gaussian tail bound, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\exists{\ell\in[L]}:|x_{\ell}^{\top}x_{q}|\geq t\mid x_{q}\right)\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbb{P}\left(|x_{\ell}^{\top}x_{q}|\geq t\mid x_{q}\right)\leq 2L\cdot\exp\left(-{t^{2}}/{2\|x_{q}\|^{2}_{2}}\right).

Hence,
\max_{\ell\in[L]}|x_{\ell}^{\top}x_{q}|\leq\sqrt{12d}\cdot\log(8L/\delta)
with probability greater than
1-\delta/4
under good event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
.
Similarly, we have
y_{\ell}=\beta^{\top}x_{\ell}+\epsilon\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,\|\beta\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma^{2})
.
Based on Lemma
E.1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\|y\|_{2}^{2}>L\cdot(\|\beta\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma^{2})\cdot(1+t)^{2}\mid\beta\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-Lt^{2}/2),

Following this, under good event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, with probability greater than
1-\delta/4
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}y_{\ell}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big(1+\sigma^{2}+5d^{-1/2}\cdot\log(4/\delta)\big)\cdot\big(1+5L^{-1/2}\cdot\log(4/\delta)\big)

\displaystyle=1+\sigma^{2}+{\rm poly}(d^{-1/2},L^{-1/2},\log(1/\delta)).

Furthermore, since
x_{\ell}^{\top}x_{q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,\|x_{q}\|_{2}^{2})
and
\mathbb{P}(x_{\ell}^{\top}x_{q}>0\mid x_{q})=1/2
for all
x_{q}
, then using the binomial tail bound in Lemma
E.2
, we can show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{L}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\ind(x_{\ell}^{\top}x_{q}>0)-\frac{1}{2}\Big|<t/2\mid x_{q}\Big)\leq 2\exp(-t^{2}L/4),

and hence
|\frac{1}{L}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\ind(x_{\ell}^{\top}x_{q}>0)-\frac{1}{2}|\leq\min\{L^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\log(8/\delta)},1/2\}
with probability at least
1-4\delta
. Combining all the arguments above, we can complete the proof.
∎

E.3
Proof of Anisotropic Pre-Conditioned GD Estimator

For simplicity, we consider a symmetric pre-conditioning matrix
\Gamma
which proved to be the global optimal parametrization for pre-conditioned GD, which can be extended to the general case with more careful arguments
(
Ahn et al., 2023a
, e.g.,)
.

Lemma E.4
.

Suppose
\beta\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d}/d)
,
X=[x_{1},\dots,x_{L}]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times d}
with
x_{\ell}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma)
and
y_{\ell}=\beta^{\top}x_{\ell}+\epsilon_{\ell}
with
\epsilon_{\ell}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
.
For any
\Gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, define
\widehat{y}_{q}^{\sf vgd}:=\widehat{y}_{q}^{\sf vgd}(\Gamma)=L^{-1}\cdot(X\beta+\epsilon)^{\top}X\Gamma^{-1}x_{q}
.
Consider the ICL prediction risk
\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{y}_{q}^{\sf vgd}-\widehat{y}_{q})^{2}]
as a function of
\Gamma
.
Then, the minimizer of
\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{y}_{q}^{\sf vgd}-\widehat{y}_{q})^{2}]
is given by

\Gamma^{*}=\left(L+1\right)/L\cdot\Sigma+(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\Sigma)+d\sigma^{2})/L\cdot I_{d}.

Proof of Lemma
E.4
.

For any matrix
\Gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, and the risk of corresponding estimator follows

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{y}_{q}^{\sf vgd}-\widehat{y}_{q})^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{L}(X\beta+\epsilon)^{\top}X\Gamma^{-1}x_{q}-\beta^{\top}x_{q}\right)^{2}\right]+\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\cdot\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\left((X^{\top}X\Gamma^{-1}/L-I_{d})^{\top}(X^{\top}X\Gamma^{-1}/L-I_{d})\Sigma\right)\right]}_{\displaystyle\textbf{(i)}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{L^{2}}\cdot\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\Gamma^{-1}X^{\top}X\Gamma^{-1}\Sigma)]}_{\displaystyle\textbf{(ii)}}+\sigma^{2}.

(E.17)

Following the decomposition above, by simple calculations, the second term follows

\displaystyle\textbf{(ii)}=\mathbb{E}[\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X^{\top}X\Gamma^{-1}\Sigma\Gamma^{-1})]=L\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\left((\Sigma\Gamma^{-1})^{2}\right).

(E.18)

Furthermore, note that the first term takes the form

(i)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\Sigma)-\frac{2}{L}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X^{\top}X\Gamma^{-1}\Sigma)]+\frac{1}{L^{2}}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\Gamma^{-1}(X^{\top}X)^{2}\Gamma^{-1}\Sigma)]

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\Sigma)-2\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\Sigma\Gamma^{-1}\Sigma)+\frac{1}{L^{2}}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}((X^{\top}X)^{2}\Gamma^{-1}\Sigma\Gamma^{-1})],

(E.19)

where the second equation follows
X^{\top}X=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}x_{\ell}x_{\ell}^{\top}
and we can show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(X^{\top}X)^{2}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}[(x_{\ell}x_{\ell}^{\top})^{2}]+\sum_{1\leq\ell\neq j\leq L}\mathbb{E}[x_{\ell}x_{\ell}^{\top}\cdot x_{j}x_{j}^{\top}]=L\cdot\mathbb{E}[(x_{\ell}x_{\ell}^{\top})^{2}]+L(L-1)\cdot\Sigma^{2}.

Specifically, for all
(i,j)\in[d]\times[d]
, the
(i,j)
-th entry of the first matrix satisfies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(x_{\ell}x_{\ell}^{\top})^{2}_{i,j}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[x_{\ell,i}x_{\ell,j}]\cdot\mathbb{E}[x_{\ell,k}^{2}]+2\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[x_{\ell,i}x_{\ell,k}]\cdot\mathbb{E}[x_{\ell,j}x_{\ell,k}]=\Sigma_{ij}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\Sigma)+\Sigma_{:,i}^{\top}\Sigma_{:,j},

where the first inequality uses the Isserlis’ theorem and thus we have
\mathbb{E}[(x_{\ell}x_{\ell}^{\top})^{2}]=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\Sigma)\Sigma+2\Sigma^{2}
.
For notational simplicity, denote
\widetilde{\Gamma}=\Sigma^{-1}\Gamma
. Combining (
E.17
), (
E.18
) and (
E.19
), it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{y}_{q}^{\sf vgd}-\widehat{y}_{q})^{2}]

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}+\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\Sigma\big)}{d}-\frac{2}{d}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\Sigma\widetilde{\Gamma}^{-1})+\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\Sigma)+d\sigma^{2}}{dL}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{-2}\big)+\frac{L+1}{dL}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\Sigma\widetilde{\Gamma}^{-2}\big)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}+\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\Sigma)}{d}-\frac{2}{d}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\Sigma\widetilde{\Gamma}^{-1}\big)+\frac{1}{dL}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\left\{(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\Sigma)+d\sigma^{2})\cdot I_{d}+(L+1)\cdot\Sigma\right\}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{-2}\big).

Since
(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\Sigma)+d\sigma^{2})\cdot I_{d}+(L+1)\cdot\Sigma
is symmetric and invertible, the minimizer is given by

\widetilde{\Gamma}^{*,-1}=L\cdot\left\{(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\Sigma)+d\sigma^{2})\cdot I_{d}+(L+1)\cdot\Sigma\right\}^{-1}\Sigma,\text{~~s.t.~~}\Gamma^{*}=\left(1+\frac{1}{L}\right)\Sigma+\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\Sigma)+d\sigma^{2}}{L}\cdot I_{d},

due to the quadratic form and then we complete the proof .
∎
